Acknowledgment


Shellharbour City Council acknowledges the Traditional Custodians of Dharawal
Country and recognises their continued connection to the land. We pay our
respects to Elders past, present and emerging and the contribution they make to
the life of this city.=== PAGE 3 ===

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     Audited Financial Statements=== PAGE 4 ===
    About
    this report



    This Annual Report provides an overview of Shellharbour City Council’s
    operational   and   financial   performance   during   the   period   1   July   2022   to   30   June
    2023.      In      particular,      this      report      focuses      on      Council’s      work      and      achievements      in
    implementing the strategies detailed in our four year Delivery Program 2022-2026.

    These strategies and actions are developed by Council in response to the
    expectations of the community (resulting from extensive community engagement)
    expressed    as    objectives    in    our    10    year    Community    Strategic    Plan    (CSP)    2022-
    2032.          These          objectives          are          summarised          into          four          focus          areas          reflecting          the
    priorities of the local community – Community, Environment, Economy and
    Leadership.      In      summary,      the      strategies      are      how      Council      will      achieve      what      the
    community needs and wants.

    This report also contains statutory reporting as prescribed by the Local
    Government Act  1993  and  Local  Government  (General)  Regulation  2005  as  well
    as other legislation for which Council is required to report annually. The statutory
    reporting includes audited financial reports providing details of Council’s financial
    performance for the year.

    The objective of the Annual Report is to:
    •    Communicate our vision and commitments to the community
         as set out in our Community Strategic Plan 2022-2032
    •    Report on our performance in implementing our strategies and achievements
    •    Demonstrate our ability to lead with confidence and deliver on our promises
    •    Recognise the significant achievements of our staff

    This information is for our community and customers – our residents, ratepayers,
    local business owners, community groups and government bodies as well as our
    current and future staff. The report also informs and engages our staff telling the
    story of how we have performed throughout the year and how their efforts have
    contributed to achieving our community vison.

    More information about our Integrated Planning
    and Reporting framework including current as well as
    past plans and reports, is available on Council’s website
    www.shellharbour.nsw.gov.au/council/news-and-publications

                                     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023      7=== PAGE 8 ===
    Who we are
    Our place,
    Our people



    Shellharbour      City      is      an      expanding      urban      area      with      significant      rural      areas      and      some      industrial      and      commercial
    land     uses.    The     City     encompasses     a     total     land     area     of     about     147     square          kilometres,     including     beaches,     rivers,
    National and State parks and other significant parklands. The first Aboriginal inhabitants of the Shellharbour area are
    comprised of clan and tribal groups of the Dharawal Nation. The main urban centres are Shellharbour City Centre,
    Shell Cove, Shellharbour Village, Albion Park, Warilla and Oak Flats. Most of the rural areas are in the south and
    west, with rural land used mainly for dairy farming and grazing.

    During    the    1990s,    growth    took    place    particularly    in    the    eastern    areas    of    the    City,    around    the    suburbs    of    Warilla,
    Mount Warrigal, Barrack Point and Barrack Heights, however, within the last ten years, growth has been greatest in
    the new release areas of Calderwood, Tullimbar and Shell Cove.


























































8     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 9 ===
It   is   expected   that   population    changes   in   the   future   will   be   influenced    by
two key factors. The older established suburbs such as Warilla and Oak
Flats will undergo a new cycle of population growth. Development will
continue within Shell Cove (particularly the Waterfront Precinct), Tullimbar,
Calderwood and parts of Shellharbour City Centre.

              Our Council




              Shellharbour City Council comprises nine elected members who
              represent the community.

              The elected Council sets the overall direction for the City through
              long-term     planning     and     decision     making.     It     adopts     a     strategic
              view of the future it wishes to achieve for its community and

Who we are
Our Values,
Our Vision






                                                             Shellharbour City Council’s Core Values provide the
                                                             foundation for our organisational culture and the way
                                                             in which we interact with, and provide services to, our
                                                             Community, Councillors, Customers and Council.

                                                             This includes guiding the way in which we deliver the
                                                             strategies and actions outlined in this document.

                                                             Our    organisational    vision    –    Inspiring    People,    Enabling
                                                             Growth provides staff with an aspirational statement
                                                             about Council’s future that connects to these Core
                                                             Values and is used as a tool in decision making and
                                                             planning for the future.

   Who we are
   Our 4C's




   The  Community  Strategic  Plan  seeks  to  provide  benefits  to  four  key  stakeholders  -  Community,  Councillors,
   Customers and the Council (the 4C’s).  Everything Council does and the actions we take to is to the benefit of
   one or more of the 4C’s.

24     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 25 ===
Year in review
Awards and Recognition


Waterfront Shell Cove - Best Regional Development Award

The     multi-award-winning    The     Waterfront,     Shell     Cove     has     added     another     prestigious     honour     to     its     lengthy     list     of
accolades,      picking      up      the      'Excellence      in      NSW      Regional      Development    Award',      at      the      UDIA      NSW      Crown      Group
Awards for Excellence.

The honour reinforces the position of The Waterfront, Shell Cove, by Shellharbour City Council with Frasers Property
Australia,    not    only    as    the    most    significant    new    marine    tourism    destination    in   Australia,    but    also    one    of    the    most
significant and impressive developments anywhere in the state.
Engineering Excellence Award for the Depot Services Building

The  new  Depot  Services  Building  at  Oak  Flats  was  awarded  the  Institute  of  Public  Works  Engineering Australasia
(IPWEA) NSW ‘Excellence in Engineering Award.’ The award recognised the excellence of Local Government and
Public Works through inspiration, innovation, collaboration, development and completion of projects and technical
management.
Meritorious Service Award

Six of our lifeguards were involved in a major incident at Warilla Beach where a young boy was struck by lightning. For
their efforts they were presented with the Meritorious Service Award from the Australian Pool and Ocean Lifeguard
Association (APOLA) in recognition of the dangerous nature of the rescue, bravery and outstanding application of
life-saving  rescue  and  first  aid  skills  which  preserved  the  life  of  the  young  boy. The  collaboration  between  Council
lifeguards and emergency services during the incident is to be commended.









































                                                                                 Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023      25=== PAGE 26 ===
    Year in review
    Awards and Recognition


    Finalist - NSW Crown Land Management Excellence Award

    Council was awarded as finalist in the Awards Australia – Community Achievement Awards in the category of 'NSW
    Crown Land Management Excellence Award'.                                                                                                 Mayor Homer and Council staff representatives attended the awards
    night which was held in the Blue Mountains.

    The NSW Department of Crown Lands nominated Shellharbour City Council for the award as a Council Crown
    Land   Manager.   Reddall   Reserve,   Lake   Illawarra   was   showcased   due   to   the   recent   improvements   made   through
    the implementation of the Reddall Reserve Masterplan and the popularity of Reddall Reserve for public art, leisure
    activities and community events.
    Shell Cove Development shortlisted

    The Shell Cove development was shortlisted for three awards being:

    •    The 2023 National Awards for Local Government in the category of excellence in ‘Regional Growth’ for the Shell
         Cove Project
    •    The    2023    Local    Government    Professionals    Excellence   Award    for    ‘Asset    and    Infrastructure    valued    over    $1.5
         million’ for The Marina Services Centre, Shell Cove
    •    The       2023       Property       Council       of     Australia       Excellence     Award       for       ‘Development       Innovation’       for      The       Waterfront
         development at Shell Cove

    The    shortlisting    is    recognition    that    the    Shell    Cove    project    has    provided    over    $2    billion    dollars    of    investment    and
    maintained  over  85%  local  employment  during  its  decades  of  planning  and  development. The  recognition  of  these
    projects amongst industry peers is very pleasing as it demonstrates the high quality of community and social
    infrastructure being delivered at Shell Cove.
    Finalist – NSW Top Tourism Town Awards 2023
    – Town Population greater than 5,000

    The NSW Top Tourism Town Awards provide communities, business chambers, visitor centres and local governments
    the chance to show how their town encourages tourism development, increases visitation and drives the visitor
    economy  for  our  state.  Shellharbour  was  a  finalist  in  the  2023  NSW Top Tourism Town Awards    – Town  population
    greater than 5,000 category, competing against 12 other finalists. Participants navigated a challenging and fiercely
    competitive process, submitting exceptional editorial pieces, captivating itineraries, and compelling videos. Their
    entries showcased unique locations, engaging activities, cultural landmarks, and locally crafted food and beverages
    with the goal of boosting the visitor economy and fostering positive social and economic impacts within the community.
    Finalist - Waste Expo Awards

    Council was a finalist at the Waste Expo Awards in Melbourne for the Little Green Bag project. This initiative involved
    distributing    a    complimentary    six-month    supply    of    compostable    bags    to    every    residential    address    in    the    City.   The
    project aimed to elevate the food organic resource recovery rate, enhance participation, and raise awareness about
    compostable bags. Council received recognition in the category of 'Community Engagement Success of the Year',
    an award that acknowledges programs or initiatives with measurable success in engaging and communicating with
    the community, leading to positive outcomes for the industry.








26     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 27 ===
Year in review
Service Reviews



Improving customer service
Council's Customer Service Charter outlines standards used to deliver professional, reliable and consistent customer
service that meets customer expectations. The experience of both the customer and community and the service
they can expect from Council is important. A set of Service Standards outline timeframes for delivery of a range of
services, along with response times in relation to customer requests.

In   2022-2023,   Council   continued   to   deliver   exceptional   customer   service   to   the   community   with   the   handling   over
31,000 calls, 12,000 face to face discussions and registering over 66,000 documents in the contact centre.

New ways to access Council services were introduced including:

•    The   use   of   the   NSW   Planning   Portal   for   applications   such   as   Development Applications,   Building   Information
     Certificates and Planning Proposal Applications
•    A telephone call-back service
•    Additional resources included the new online platform ‘Discover Shellharbour ’ which provides access to over
     9,000 items from the Shellharbour City Museum

This year saw the commencement of Council’s efforts to develop a Service Review Framework, expanding upon
earlier    initiatives.   This    framework    takes    into    account    Council's    efficiency,    productivity,    financial    sustainability,    and
governance.

Comprising of four essential components, Council's Service Review Framework integrates the service review
program, audits, business plans, and business improvement.

Recognising the legislative requirement for all Council’s to identify and assess their services, particular emphasis
was placed on developing a dedicated service review program that enables Council the opportunity to review its
services, service levels, and resources, ensuring they remain sustainable, relevant, contemporary, and in alignment
with community expectations and values.

A service catalogue will be used to review priority services over the 2022-2026 Delivery Program, with each service
review resulting in an improvement plan to adjust service levels or improve efficiency where required.

Business improvement and efficiency
Council has a strong commitment to continuous improvement, In recent years, Council has focused on embedding
business improvement into its way of working.  This year saw Council implement a number of improvement initiatives
aimed at enhancing efficiency and optimising the use of existing resources.

This included the:

•    Development of meeting and email protocols across the organisation
•    Review of Councils asset handover process
•    Simplification of the Project Management Framework
•    Induction process for contractors and volunteers
•    Review of delegations across the organisation

   Our Achievements
   2022/23



   In this section of the Annual Report, Council’s performance is outlined
   in more detail focusing on outcomes achieved against each strategy
   listed in our Delivery Program 2022 – 2026 taking into account the

    Council’s Public Art Strategy enables and cultivates a collection of inspiring, accessible                        Operational
    and   dynamic   public   art   experiences   fostering   creativity   throughout   the   City.   In   line   with Expense
    the strategy, Council continued its ‘Art in Unexpected Places’ program. This temporary
    art installation is displayed for three months and during the year featured five artists.
    A    professional    development    program    for    artists    was    established    at    the    Imaginarium    in $1.515M
    Shellharbour Village. The Incubator Artists Residency program provided emerging artists
    with the dedicated space and time to cultivate new work, explore ideas and develop their
    practices.  The program provided exclusive access to the space for a four-week period,                            Capital
    as well as a $5,000 allowance towards fees and materials.                                                         Spend

    Artists from the Coomaditchie United Aboriginal Corporation completed three artworks
    for   the   new   Oak   Flats   Services   Depot   building.  The   commissioned   artworks   reflect   the       $506k
    buildings purpose as a place to meet with community, discuss ideas and make decisions,
    as well as being inspired by the Dharawal names given to the meeting rooms in the
    building.

    Council’s libraries fostered partnerships with the community through its delivery of over                         Total performance
    1,080       diverse       programs.       Libraries       promoted       Plastic       Free       July,       Library       &       Information
    Week and History Week while the monthly genealogy sessions, ‘Family History Help’,                                progress met
    and monthly craft workshops ‘Everybody Makes’ were well attended.

    Public programs for young people proved popular including school holiday programs and
    a series of programs to support HSC students. This included an HSC English information
    session, a Lock-In Study and Pizza Night and the creation of a cosey and practical study                                  100%
    space for students at City Library.

    Other highlights included, the celebration of Book Week Author Talks, the StoryTrail in
    the Civic Square featuring the story ‘Christmas Wombat’ by Jackie French to encourage
    reading in an outdoor setting. Albion Park Library hosted its first ‘Live @ the Library’ event
    that was a great success with over 100 attendees enjoying free crafts, games, live music,
    gardening, a delicious BBQ and even a visit from Santa.

    In addition to the library’s regular early literacy programs (Babies Love Books, Storytime
    and My First STEM) new children’s programs were introduced at Warilla Library including
    ‘Positive Play’ and ‘Craft with Me’ and a monthly ‘Crafternoon’ program at Oak Flats
    Library.

    Shellharbour Libraries have also been actively involved in outreach programs with a pop-
    up library at the Collins Reserve opening and a targeted weekly program at Albion Park
    Library to engage and connect with young people.






















30     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 31 ===
Council supported and implemented a number of key activations that brought people together from across the City.
Events included:

Commemorative Service for Her Majesty Queen Elizabeth II
A     special     commemorative     service     was     held     at     the     Civic     Centre     for     Her     Majesty     Queen     Elizabeth     II.     Community
members were invited to sign the book of condolence and lay flowers/wreaths.

Carols by Candlelight and Random Acts of Carols
Carols by Candlelight was held at the new location of Albion Park Showground. The free event was a huge success,
with over 6,000 guests in attendance for a night of song, dance, circus performers and delightful surprises. The event
saw the inclusion of Auslan interpreters, making the it a more inclusive and accessible experience for all.

The introduction of ‘Random Acts of Carols’ conducted by the Shellharbour City Chorale, saw Christmas cheer
spread at Little Park Rotunda in Shellharbour Village and at Boonerah Point, Oak Flats.

Symphony by the Sea
Symphony by the Sea was held at Reddall Reserve with the event featuring The Sydney Youth Orchestra (SYO)
and the SYO Philharmonic Orchestra. Despite the adverse impact of severe weather conditions on the event, both
attendees and artists provided overwhelmingly positive feedback.

International Women’s Day
Council    hosted    the    International    Women’s    Day    event    at    Shellharbour    Civic    Centre.   The    free    event    invited    young
women aged 16-25 years old to enjoy breakfast and discuss the year ’s theme – ‘Embrace Equity’. A panel of women
pioneering in their space, were invited to share what the theme meant to them. Motivational speaker Kath Koshcel,
from The Kindness Factory also attended the event, sharing her story and answering questions from the audience.




















































                                                                                    Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023      31=== PAGE 32 ===
    Enchanted Forest
    Over  66,300  guests  immersed  themselves  in  Shellharbour ’s  own  unique,  dazzling,  and  award-winning  light  show,
    that featured vibrant installations, dazzling laser effects, interactive projections, and magical holograms. The family-
    friendly   event   was   a   partnership   with   LASERVISION,   the   world’s   leading   creator   and   producer   of   light,   water   and
    sound show.

    With  a  focus  to  increase  access  and  inclusion  to  events,  low-sensory  sessions  were  offered  every  night,  designed
    for people with specific sensory needs or who prefer a more subdued environment. These sessions featured smaller
    crowds and a lower-intensity light display with reduced sound levels and were booked out across the 24 nights.

    The Enchanted Forest took visitors on a journey of light and sound through the heart of the native forest and
    parklands of Blackbutt Forest. This event included vibrant installations, including laser effects, interactive projection
    and holograms. An innovative mural by artist Happy Decay added to this experience, using UV paint to bring to life
    ‘Folk of the Forest’ during night and day for the duration of the event.

issues including children and families, young people, seniors, people with disability, First
Nations people, multicultural community, health, wellbeing and safety.                                            $1.677M

Financial   assistance   is   provided   under   S365   of   the   NSW   Local   Government Act   1993.
Council allocates funds to develop volunteer projects and individual aspirations, which are
in accordance with Council’s objectives outlined in the Financial Assistance – Donation’s                         Capital
Policy.                                                                                                           Spend

Council delivered services to young people throughout the City in line with the Youth
Action Plan.   These services are delivered throughout the City including at the Blackbutt
Youth Centre and Albion Park Youth Space, with a range of youth-based activations held                             Total performance
across various locations in both the school term and school holidays.

Youth Summit
Council held its annual Youth Summit with over 60 young people attending. The summit
is a key initiative of the Shellharbour Youth Council providing an opportunity for young
people to connect, have a voice and network with local leaders.

Youth Council member Gabe, was Master of Ceremony ensuring the smooth running of
the event. Optimum Experience facilitated a morning of ice breakers and local duo Lemon
Pepper performed throughout the day. During the afternoon, discussion workshops were
conducted    to    explore    the    topics    within    the   Youth    Plan    -    Live,    Grow    and    Play    -    to    start
consultation on what Council needs to focus on in the new plan.

























                                                                                   Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023      33=== PAGE 34 ===
    Library Resources
    Shellharbour City Libraries introduced
    additional culturally relevant resources
    to enhance young people’s knowledge
    of First Nation’s language and culture.
    The new books expand on the Library’s
    Aboriginal Culture collection.

    The       new       resources       include       the       ground-
    breaking ‘Dharawal Words, Phrases and
    Activities’ book compiled and edited by
    Dr     Jodi     Edwards.     This     is     an     extensively
    researched resource on Dharawal
    language,     specifically     for     Young     People.
    The book takes young readers on
    a journey in learning the Dharawal
    language through enchanting illustrations
    and fun activities.

    ‘Our Yarning’ is another series of
    resources      created      by      and      for    Aboriginal      and     Torres      Strait      Islander      peoples.     These      early      readers      aim      to      inspire
    and engage Aboriginal young people by providing access to stories and reading material that mirror their own
    experiences and cultural identity. The new books are available at all of Council’s libraries.

    Your Library Online
    In    a    major    refresh    of    the    24/7    Digital    Library,    Shellharbour    City    Libraries    introduced    a    suite    of    new    online    library
    products targeting a variety of learning interests and age groups. For young children, there are three new engaging
    products:  10  Monkeys,  Literacy  Planet  and  Rainforest  Coding.  Each  product  supports  a  fun,  safe  and  motivational
    online environment that encourages children to learn at their own pace.

    For students undertaking their HSC, Shellharbour was one of the first public libraries in NSW to offer HSC4Me. This
    product provides students from our local high schools with academic support through webinars, videos, practice
    HSC exam questions, notes and live classrooms with experienced senior HSC teachers and markers.  HSC4Me
    offers the opportunity for improved results and reduced anxiety for local students requiring only a library membership
    for access to the exceptional resource.

    Creativebug, is the latest lifelong learning product to be offered to our community, with a vibrant online art and craft
    learning experience that supports the library’s creative collections and programs. Thousands of free online classes
    are suited for beginners exploring the latest techniques through to advanced creatives wanting to refresh their
    crafting skills. The product offers content for children and adults and is available free to library members.

    A Seed Library at Oak Flats
    Shellharbour City Libraries continued its commitment to support ‘green living’ with the launch of the Seed Library at
    Oak Flats Library. The Seed Library aims to encourage people to grow healthy food, reduce costs, and become more
    active in the garden. The Seed Library offers a selection of seasonal seeds including vegetables, herbs and flowers
    which can be ‘borrowed’ free of charge by library members. Customers can borrow up to two packets of seeds from
    Oak Flats Library to grow in their own gardens. After growing, members can harvest and return seeds to the Seed
    Library to share with other community members.

    The Seed Library is built on a foundation of sharing, strengthening biodiversity and food security for our local
    community. The program presents a great opportunity for young people to learn about the life cycle of plants and
    develop  seed-saving  skills.   To  support  the  Seed  Library  initiative,  additional  books  and  marketing  resources  were
    provided to help customers grow and collect seeds, continuing the theme of sustainability. The program received an
    enthusiastic response from local residents.

    Reconciliation
    Reconciliation is achieved through a variety of actions, one of which is by ensuring local Aboriginal culture and
    heritage is respected and protected and that local Aboriginal communities are consulted on relevant land use matters.

    Council supported engagement with the local Aboriginal communities that included the development of an Aboriginal
    Culture and Heritage Assessment for the Bass Point Reserve as well as a Plan of Management for Reddall Reserve,
    both of which are sites of substantial Aboriginal cultural and heritage significance.

    In      June,      Council      formally      adopted      and      endorsed      a      formal      Reflect      Reconciliation    Action      Plan      (RAP),      which      was
    developed in partnership with Reconciliation Australia (the peak body for promoting, supporting and implementing
    reconciliation actions and strategies), along with support from Councillors and the Aboriginal Advisory Committee
    (AAC).

    In   addition   to   these   strategic   strides   towards   reconciliation,   Council   also   hosted   the   annual   Reconciliation   School
    Flag      Walk      at      Warilla      Surf      Club      and      Reddall      Reserve.     The      Reconciliation      Flag      Walk      was      attended      by      over      600
    students, as well as teaching staff, community members and local service providers. The event was supported by
    special guests including Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander Elders and community members, Councillors, Ministers,
    AAC members and representatives from Council’s Executive Leadership Team. The community came together to
    celebrate and reflect upon the importance of reconciliation.

    NAIDOC week
    Council    successfully    celebrated    the    opening    of    NAIDOC    week    by    delivering    the    NAIDOC    Community    Day    at    the
    Civic Centre. Approximately 500 people attended the event, opening with a cultural performance by Gumaraa and a
    Welcome to Country by Uncle Richard Davis.

    Attendees  were  treated  to  performances,  with  20  service  providers  supplying   the  community  with  information   and
    fun activities. Local Aboriginal artist Hannah Jensen facilitated a live paint across the day. There are plans to display
    this artwork across the City’s libraries and community centres.

                                                                                Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023      37=== PAGE 38 ===
    Child Safety and Wellbeing Policy
    To deliver on the Child Safe Standards outlined by the NSW Government, Council created a new Child Safety and
    Wellbeing Policy. The policy outlines Council’s commitment to keeping children and young people safe from harm
    while interacting with Council services. It sets out the value of safety and wellbeing of all children and young people
    in the community. The policy is a foundational step towards becoming a child safe organisation.

    The community can also now access Child Safety information on Council’s website that shares information about
    Council’s Child Safe journey and projects. The page explains the key points of the policy and Council’s commitment
    to     Child     Safety     in     an     easy-to-understand     format.    The     frequently     asked     questions     section     also     outlines     Council’s
    vision for improvement, what impact the project will have and how community members can find help if they need it.

    Disability Access and Inclusion Plan
    The ‘Draft Disability Access and Inclusion Plan: 2023-2026 An Inclusive
    City’ was presented to Council and placed on public exhibition. Following
    the public exhibition period, the Plan will be presented to Council again
    for adoption.

    The Plan sets the direction to make Shellharbour City a place where
    people with disabilities and their families are able to participate in all
    elements of life as equals. The Plan sets out Council’s priorities and
    actions for the next three years, and builds on the success of its current
    Plan and provides an opportunity for further improvement across the
    existing four focus areas:

    •    Developing positive community attitudes
    •    Creating livable communities
    •    Supporting access to meaningful employment and
    •    Improving access to services through better systems and processes

    The Plan is supported by an Easy Read version that presents the key information in a format that makes it easy to
    read and understand.

    Aged Friendly Action Plan
    Council contributes to a City that is age friendly through the delivery of
    a range of services and initiatives aimed to recognise and celebrate the
    achievements of older people.

    Dying to Know Day
    Dying    to    Know    Day    (D2KD)    was    held    with    the    purpose    of    the    event    to
    encourage conversations to improve knowledge and choices around end
    of life. This year saw the launch of the Healthy End of Life Project short
    films.

    Four people talked about their experiences with end of life, death and
    dying and how these experiences have enriched and added to the way
    they view and live their lives. Other activities held on the day included a
    special author talk by Doctor Dominic Frawley on his book ‘Malachy – A
    Father ’s Story of Love, Laughter and Loss’ and a panel discussion ‘You
    CAN ask that!’ on death and dying.



















38     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 39 ===
Seniors Festival 2023
The 2023 Seniors Festival saw activities targeted to engage community
members over the age of 50. The festival was launched at the Health and
Wellbeing    Expo    with    over    350    people    entertained    by    four    performance
groups and 25 stalls.

There were also a range of workshops and activities, spanning from
yoga,   tie-dyeing,   brain   games   programs,   craft   and   art   workshops   held,
with good attendance. The festival also saw Frasers and Council deliver
an informative ‘Marina Walking Tour ’ on the Shell Cove development.

The festival was well supported by local organisations and provided
older people in our community with the opportunity to get together, learn
new things and have fun.



























Cultural Diversity Action Plan
In  support  of  harmony  and  unity  among  all  cultures,  Council  held  a  series  of  Harmony  Day  activities  that  included
Indian     henna     hand     painting,     Greek    cooking     workshop     and     a    Mexican     themed     community     film.    Council     hosted     a
World Humanitarian Day screening the Scattered People film, to bring awareness and celebrate the value and rich
culture of refugees from diverse backgrounds living in Australia. This multi-award winning documentary film featured
world famous Australian musicians bringing hope to refugees in detention.

‘Get the Site Right’
Council participated in the ‘Get the Site Right’ blitz day with the NSW Environment Protection Authority. Council was
invited to present an information session to a local builder in order to educate their trades in responsible and proper
site management, particularly around erosion and sediment control. Engagement exercises like these help establish
control and guidance to protect the City's streets and waterways.

40     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 41 ===
A draft Community Wellbeing Action Plan was developed that includes a number of strategies and actions across the
four themes of Eat Well, Move Well, Connect Well and Think Well. Extensive community and stakeholder engagement
was      undertaken      that     included      community      pop-up      activities,      wellbeing      workshops      and      ‘Walk      and     Talk’     meetings.
Council   continues   to   build   and   foster   partnerships   with   other   organisations,   including   Healthy   Cities   Illawarra   and
Illawarra Shoalhaven Local Health District to work collaboratively on a range of initiatives.

Implementation, monitoring and review of the Open Space and Recreation Strategy continued. The Strategy outlines
Council’s ten-year plan to guide decision making in respect to planning, investment and use of its open space and
recreational facilities. It covers both active recreation, such as organised sports, as well as passive activities, such
as walking or enjoying nature. Key achievements included:

•     Opening of Reddall Reserve all-inclusive play space
•     Opening of Collins Reserve Sensory Garden
•     Opening of the Aboriginal Interpretive Playspace - Yirran muru
•     Continuation of the Dogs on Beaches project
•     Continuation of work on the future recreation projects at McDonald Park Netball Courts, Dawes Park, Clermont
      Park, Bardsley Park and Jones Park Warilla Dog Agility Park
•     Continuation of investigations into the potential for synthetic sportsfields
•     Commenced community engagement for the Tullimbar Oval Masterplan and Harrison Park Youth Space
•     Drafting of a new Shade in Playspaces Policy
•     Commencement of Oakleigh Park sportsfield masterplan project
•     Adoption of The Shellharbour City Community Gardens Strategy

The Shellharbour City Community Gardens Strategy     was     adopted     by     Council     in     December     2022     that     stemmed
from    the    Shellharbour    Open    Space    and    Recreation    Needs    Study    and    Strategy    (2020)    that    identified    an    ongoing
demand within the community for the provision of community gardens. It provides a strategic focus for establishing
and expanding the Community Gardens network across the City and was prepared in consultation with existing
community garden groups and the wider community.

Council        continued        to        manage        all        property        occupation        agreements        and        bookings        associated        with        Sportsfields,
Community   Centres   and   Halls   with   over   1,200   registered   requests   received   during   the   year.  There   were   167   new
applications for temporary licences requesting the use of Council lands for events such as civil ceremonies, access
over Council land and filming permits.

42     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 43 ===
Opening of Services Depot Building
The  new  Services  Depot  Building  was  officially  opened  in August. The  project  stems  from  many  years  of  planning
and   design   with   the   aim   of   providing   appropriate   facilities   for   more   than   150   staff   that   work   across   the   depot   site
including    civil    works,    parks,    store,    workshop,    nursery,    IT,    HR    and    administration    staff.   The    new    building    features
meeting    and    training    rooms,    hot    desks    and    is    a    huge    improvement    on    the    40-year-old    facilities    that    the    building
replaces.

The upgrade also allows for future growth to enable essential services to be delivered to the City’s growing community.

   Shellharbour Rocks Series
   Shellharbour Rocks was the largest community music and arts festival series ever
   produced by Shellharbour City Council.  The Shellharbour Rocks Series was funded                         Capital
   using significant grant funding received from NSW Government Reconnecting Regional
   Community Events Program.  The Series consisted of a number of unique events that                        Spend
   were held across the City bringing together the character of Town Centres.

   Shellharbour Rocks the Park was planned as a country and blues themed festival                           -
   however due to extreme weather conditions this event was unfortunately cancelled and
   Shellharbour Rocks Unplugged was introduced, a series of smaller pop up activations
   across Albion Park on 25 February.

   Shellharbour Rocks the Village festival featured a dynamic blend of surf, skate, and                     Total performance
   music, showcasing a main stage with seven acts, pop-up musicians stationed in six small                  progress met
   businesses along the main street, four bands performing at the Ocean Beach Hotel, as
   well as thrilling skate and surf competitions.

   Shellharbour Rocks the Flats was a Street Performance themed festival held in Oak Flats
   town centre. This free family-friendly event featured a line-up of internationally renowned,
   hilarious and highly-skilled street theatre artists, magicians and comedians.                                    100%

   Shellharbour Rocks the Lake festival showcased Aboriginal artists, performers and
   creatives. The  event  was  held  at  Reddall  Reserve  and  featured  Indigenous  performers,
   artists and creatives for a day of music, creative workshops, and storytelling.

   Shellharbour Rocks The Marina – Unplugged was a blues and folk festival in Shell Cove.
   The event kept everyone entertained with smooth blues tunes, live folk music, free family
   friendly activities including a farmyard nursery, face painting, roving circus performances
   and market stalls.

Civic Centre Activation
Council continued to activate the Shellharbour Civic Centre and provided a range of unique and quality experiences
to   the   community.   The Aboriginal   Interpretive   Playspace   -   Yirran   muru   -   was   officially   opened   to   the   public   in   the
backyard of the Civic Centre on 18 April. The opening consisted of a smoking ceremony and cultural performances
by various local cultural practitioners. The playspace has been extremely popular and was recognised at the Parks
and Leisure Conference for its design excellence.


Further highlights over the 12 months included:

•    The Civic Centre Markets to celebrate and support young entrepreneur ’s during Youth Week
•    A Family Trivia Night with approximately 150 people in attendance as part of the school holiday programs
•    Hosting of five Year 6 formals along with a school awards ceremony
•    Transformation of the Civic Centre into an ‘Under the Sea’ theme for school holidays
•    Creative Series workshops, school holiday programs and regular and new bookings

                                                                                                                        and future generations, Council is preparing a Coastal Management Program (CMP)                                    Expense
                                                                                                                        under the Coastal Management Act 2016. This will transition away from Council’s current
                                                                                                                        coastal   policy,   the   Coastal   Zone   Management   Plan.  There   are   five   stages   to   preparing
                                                                                                                        and implementing a CMP. Following review and comments earlier this year, Council’s                                 $1.528M
                                                                                                                        Coastal  Management  Program  Stage  1  –  Scoping  Study  was  supported  by  the  Coastal
                                                                                                                        Management Advisory Committee (CMAC).

                                                                                                                        which   identifies   priority   issues   and   management   gaps   to   inform   the   further   preparation       Spend
                                                                                                                        of    the    CMP.    Council    also    endorsed    preliminary    planning    for    Stages    2    &    4    of    the    CMP
                                                                                                                        framework, including the recommended technical studies outlined in the Scoping Study.
                                                                                                                        Council applied for a grant with the New South Wales Environment Protection Agency                                 $682k
                                                                                                                        (NSW    EPA)    Stream    2:    Strategic    Development    and    Capacity    Building    Litter    Prevention
                                                                                                                        Grant,        for        a        value        of        $100,000.        This        grant        will        help        Council        to        build        capacity,        take
                                                                                                                        ownership and plan strategically for sustainable initiatives in litter prevention for the City.

                                                                                                                        Two BeachStat cameras were installed at Warilla Seawall and at Shell Cove Harbour                                   Total performance
                                                                                                                        Breakwater   South.   BeachStat   is   an   image-based   beach   monitoring   system,   using   low-               progress met
                                                                                                                        cost   cameras   that   are   automated   and   self-contained.  The   system   is   ideal   for   collecting
                                                                                                                        routine, high frequency timelapse imagery of beaches. Along with Council’s CoastSnap
                                                                                                                        cameras, BeachStat will contribute to our coastal monitoring program. The information
                                                                                                                        gathered from CoastSnap and BeachStat will allow Council to monitor and understand
                                                                                                                        any  long-term  changes  to  the  coastline  that  occur  as  a  result  of  the  large-scale  human                        100%
                                                                                                                        interventions.

                                                                                                                        Natural area restoration and maintenance work continued across the City, utilising best
                                                                                                                        practice Bush Regeneration techniques. These included activities such as weeding,
                                                                                                                        poisoning, mulching and planting, throughout Blackbutt, Shell Cove, Bass Point, Flinders,
                                                                                                                        Stoney Range, Croom Reserve, Coastal Dunes, Myimbarr wetlands, and other riparian
                                                                                                                        areas, as per our Plans of Management and Dune Management Plans.

                                                                                                                        Bushcare and Landcare activities continue at all of Council’s active sites, including Oakey
                                                                                                                        Creek, Blackbutt, Koona Bay, Warilla Dunes, Campaspe Watercourse and Tullimbar with
                                                                                                                        positive outcomes. Grant projects continue to meet milestones.

                                                                                                                        Council continued to investigate activities that cause harm to our built and natural
                                                                                                                        environment, including pollution, illegal dumping, overgrown and unhealthy properties

                                                                                                                        to reduce instances of illegal dumping and remain an active member of the partnership
                                                                                                                        between    Illawarra    Councils,    the    Illawarra/Shoalhaven    Regional    Illegal    Dumping    Squad
                                                                                                                        and the Environmental Protection Authority. Council continued to target known dumping
                                                                                                                        locations, install covert and overt surveillance equipment and implement measures to

                                                                                                                        illegal dumping within Croome Reserve.

    CoastSnap
    CoastSnap    stations    were    installed    at    Shell    Cove    Harbour    Breakwater,    Warilla    South    Beach,    Windang    Island    and
    Shellharbour      South      Beach.      CoastSnap      is      a      citizen      science      platform      for      beach      monitoring      that      relies      on      crowd-
    sourced imagery to be collected and submitted by community members. It is a low-cost beach monitoring technology
    that turns personal smart phones into powerful coastal monitoring devices.

    The information gathered will allow Council to monitor and understand any long-term changes to the coastline that
    occur  as  a  result  of  the  large-scale  human  interventions,  while  also  capturing  baseline  data  for  other  areas.  Using
    CoastSnap assists Council in engaging with the community and beach users, to help develop their understanding
    and value of beach processes and beach management activities.

Climate and Sustainability Policy – Zero Emissions Shellharbour                                                      Total performance
Strategy                                                                                                             progress met
On             8             November             Council             endorsed             the             first             Climate             and             Sustainability             Policy             for
Shellharbour and Zero Emissions Shellharbour Strategy 2022 – 2050.

The Climate & Sustainability Policy supports Council's Zero Emissions Shellharbour
Strategy by aligning operational and strategic Council planning towards net zero. This                                        100%
will enable us to:
•    Achieve Council’s net zero operational and community targets.
•    Build climate resilience in Shellharbour City for now and into the future.
•    Promote environmental sustainability and protect the ecological values of
     Shellharbour City for the health and wellbeing of the community.
•    Engage with the community throughout the net zero journey to educate, support and
     empower sustainable action from all Shellharbour residents.

The Zero Emissions Shellharbour Strategy   defines   our   path   toward   achieving   net   zero
greenhouse    gas    emissions    for    both    Council    and    our    community.    It    also    maps    a    series
of goals to track our progress. These include goals to address Council operational
emissions from electricity, fuel, natural gas, waste or offsetting remaining emissions. The
Strategy sets the goal for net zero by 2035 for Council’s operational emissions. Council is
also committed to supporting the community to achieve net zero emissions by 2050 and
building a resilient and adaptive community, for the long-term sustainability of the City.
                       Tackling Climate Change together

                                                                                     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023      49=== PAGE 50 ===
    Restoration of Sand Dunes at Shellharbour North and South Beaches
    Council undertook bush regeneration and restoration works in both South and North Shellharbour dune systems,
    funded through an undertaking from Sydney Water. The works will focus on restoring and rehabilitating the beach
    dunes; and preventing damage to the dunes through fencing, access improvements, and education.

    Weed coverage was reduced to 50% across the dune system at both North and South Beach locations. Council have
    conducted large planting days with over 18,529 plants installed across both dune systems. North beach location had
    had 13,597 installed and South Beach location has had 4,932 installed from the start of the project.

    Students      from    Albion      Park      High      School      and      Shellcove      Public      School      together      with      Council      planted      2,000      new
    seedlings, while learning about the restoration as part of National Tree Day.

    Council’s Nursery supported the project, assisting to grow plants for the undertaking with a total of 22,257 tubes at
    various  stages of development. The total number  of plants  required  for this project is 60,000  with 40,000  allocated
    to North Beach and 20,000 to South beach location.

Scrap Together FOGO Education
The Scrap Together FOGO education project provided residents with information on how
to live more sustainably and how to best use their household FOGO service to create a
quality FOGO product.

Information was shared through social media, website articles and newspaper advertising.
Council worked closely with the Environmental Protection Authority to ensuring a
consistent messaging with other councils in NSW to better facilitate the most sustainable
outcomes for the Shellharbour FOGO system.

    Clean Up Australia Day 2023
    As part of Clean Up Australia Day Council undertook a clean-up around Picnic Island at Lake Illawarra. During this
    clean up a total of 27.5kg of recyclables, 18kg of general waste and 40kg of metal debris was collected.

    emissions      annually.      Solar      My      School's      collaboration      extended      to      Lake      Illawarra      High

    Council reported on its tree management practices, paving the way for the forthcoming
    city-wide         greening         strategy.         Set         to         commence         in         the         third         quarter         of         this         year,         the
    Shellharbour Greening Strategy aims to ensure the sustainability and livability of
    our urban areas, streets, natural landscapes, coastlines, and open spaces for future
    generations. The strategy's focus lies in integrating nature into our urban environments,
    enhancing well-being, and fostering increased biodiversity within Shellharbour.                                                  Total performance
                                                                                                                                     progress met
    Risk-Based Framework for Stormwater Management
    In      collaboration      with      Wollongong      City      Council      and      the      NSW      Government,      Council      is
    actively     developing     a     Risk-Based     Framework     to     manage     stormwater     runoff     into     Lake
    Illawarra.  This  innovative  approach  embraces  comprehensive  catchment  management,                                                   100%
    tailoring stormwater pollutant reduction targets to address specific water quality concerns
    within the Lake and its tributaries. While the NSW Government has temporarily suspended
    this project, Council remains committed. Council is dedicated to integrating the regional
    water-sensitive     framework     into     our     operations     and     future     planning,     promoting     climate
    adaptation and resilience in the process.

    A number of core land use strategies guide the way in which Council undertakes land                                 Operational
    use planning that reflects the unique character of our City and natural environment and is                          Expense
    responsive to the evolving needs of our community. These include Plans of Management,
    the        Shellharbour        Local        Environment        Plan        (LEP)        2013        and        Local        Strategic        Planning
    Statement (LSPS).                                                                                                   $1.051M

    Plans of management categorise land, authorise leases or licenses and determine what
    development can take place. The key values of the land and its purpose are identified so
    they can be protected and enhanced. We ensure public consultation happens for plans                                 Capital
    of management before they are adopted.                                                                              Spend

    Three   Crown   reserve   plans   of   management   were   drafted   during   2022/2023   and   are   at
    varying  stages.  These  included  Little  Park  and  Shellharbour  Reserve;  the  Shellharbour                     -
    Beachside  Holiday  Park;  and  the  adjoining  reserves  of  Reddall,  Picnic  Island,  Werrang
    Island, Berageree Island, Pelican View, Graham Park and Mrs Halls Park.

    Shellharbour  LEP  2013  contains  the  zoning  and  planning  controls  that  set  out  how  and
    where development such as single houses, townhouses, shops and industrial buildings                                 Total performance
    can be built. Several planning proposals were considered throughout the year including
    rezoning of land in areas such Albion Park, Tullimbar and Dunmore.                                                  progress met

    To further guide future planning for the City the Shellharbour Rural Lands Strategy is
    being developed. The purpose of the strategy is to ensure the management of rural lands
    is sustainable and reflects community needs and values.
    Local Strategic Planning Statement                                                                                           86%

    The Local Strategic Planning Statement (LSPS) sets the land use vision for the future
    of    Shellharbour    City    Local    Government  Area.    It    provides    details    on    which    Council    can
    base planning decisions and drive future land use planning decisions and management
    of growth in the City based on our economic, social and environmental needs over the
    next 20 years.

    Council’s first LSPS was made in May 2020 and the first review was completed in 2022.
    Council continues to implement relevant short term and ongoing actions. There are a
    total      of      83      actions      in      the      LSPS      to      be      implemented      over      the      next      20      years.      Of      these
    actions, Council has initiated approximately 47 ongoing and short-term actions.

    Throughout this year, Council achieved notable progress on several LSPS actions,
    including:

    •    P6.1          Review          and          update          Council’s          Shared          Use          Path          Master          Plan          to          prioritise
         footpaths and cycle ways across the City to encourage active travel
    •    P6.2 Develop strategies to improve transport connectivity across the City
    •    P.6.5 Review Council’s planning controls to ensure that accessibility and inclusion is
         promoted throughout our built environment















56     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 57 ===
Council commenced an Active Transport Strategy which will assist in delivering these three LSPS actions. The
Strategy    will    be    a    long-term    plan    that    sets    the    vision,    objectives,    and    priorities    for    investment    in    active    transport
framework within the City.

P7.1 Provide Fast-charging user pays
infrastructure throughout the City at key
locations for electric vehicles

•    This            year,            Council            installed            two            22kw            wall
     mounted charging points in the public car park
     at the Shellharbour Civic Centre. After offering
     an initial month of free charging to customers,
     the stations are now user pays. These are
     the  first  charging  stations  installed  by  Council
     within the City.

P13.2 Prepare a Coastal Management Program
for the City’s open coast

•    The Scoping Study for the Coastal Management
     Program (CMP) for the City’s open coast was
     adopted this year. The Scoping Study is the first
     stage   in   a   five   stage   process   of   developing   a
     certified CMP. The Scoping Study identifies the
     scope for the CMP, including key management
     issues, information needs and stakeholder and
     community engagement strategy to identify
     how participation in the management of the
     coast can be undertaken.

P14.1 Preparation of a Rural Land Use Strategy
to ensure the management of rural lands is
sustainable and reflects community needs and
values

•    Council initiated the preparation of a Rural
     Land Use Strategy this year that will guide
     future planning of rural lands and management
     development within or adjoining rural areas.
     An initial round of community consultation
     was undertaken this year to help guide the
     development of the Strategy.

Council  continues  to  monitor  and  report  on  the  implementation  of  the  LSPS  through  Council's  Integrated  Planning
and Reporting Framework. Council’s Delivery Program and Operational Plan (DPOP) incorporates relevant LSPS
actions and outlines when these actions will commence. The inclusion of LSPS actions into the DPOP enables
Council to monitor and track progress of the implementation of the LSPS and ensures Council delivers on the actions
identified in the LSPS.

Employment Lands Study
In     response     to     the     Illawarra     Shoalhaven     Regional     Plan     and     Council’s     Local     Strategic Total performance
Planning Statement, the Shellharbour Employment Lands Study (SELS) was completed                                  progress met
in 2021. The Study provides recommendations on Shellharbour City’s future employment
lands’        needs;        including        for        commercial        and        industrial        zoned        land,        to        allow        for        local
employment opportunities. The SELS recognises the NSW Department of Planning &
Environment’s (DPE) current program of reforming employment land use zones and
recommends      Local      Environmental      Plan      2013      (LEP      2013)      be      reviewed      as      part      of      the 70%
reform. Four new employment zones commenced in Shellharbour Local Environmental
Plan 2013 (the commencement), replacing eight former business/industrial zones.

A meeting conducted by the DPE was attended regarding bringing existing employment
zones          in          State          Environmental          Planning          Policy          (Precincts          –          Regional)          2021          (which
includes the Calderwood housing release area) in line with the employment zones now
in    LEP    2013.    Council    also    liaised    with    the    DPE    over    the    LEP    2013    mapping    changes
associated with the reform.

Development Application Strategy is to:

     community
•    enhance the customer experience in both the process of lodging a development
     application as well as the outcome of determination

     development assessment

     framework

    Shellharbour City Stadium
    The actions outlined in the Shellharbour City Stadium (The Stadium) Business Plan were progressively implemented,
    with external grant funding being investigated to support specific infrastructure improvements. The Stadium manages
    and supports many events, competitions and programs. Including:

    •    Netball
    •    Pickleball
    •    Basketball
    •    Sports Parties
    •    Futsal / Soccer
    •    Bubble soccer
    •    Wheelchair sports
    •    Volleyball
    •    Badminton
    •    Extreme frisbee
    •    Dodgeball
    •    European handball
    •    Fitness or dance classes

    In addition to hosting its regular sporting competitions and events the stadium also hosted:

    •    an inaugural All Stars Gala Day celebrated across all our programs
    •    the NSW Craft Alive Expo, an event that has been travelling through NSW and has made Shellharbour home
         with leading craft retailers, kits, supplies and equipment
    •    the 3 x 3 Wheelchair competition which attracted competitors from Australia and New Zealand
    •    basketball development programs and the Illawarra netball trials
    •    school holidays and development programs
    •    Seniors  Week  activities  which  created  opportunities  for  community  engagement,  offering  sports  in  a  simplified
         version to the community to participate within their capabilities
    •    coaching programs across different sports. This was well received and positive feedback from our community
         and sporting groups will see this program continue and expand.

    Nursery
    The Nursery Business Plan continued to be implemented. The Nursery was open to the public on a Saturday morning
    during September, October, November and December. Weekday trading was also extended during December.

    Propagation of plant stock continued to track well to support normal operations and the significantly increased orders
    associated with natural area regeneration projects. An agreement has been reached with TAFE to use their facilities
    to grow the increased stock.

    The Nursery focused on supplying Council works and external trades customers with seed stock sourced locally and
    propagated by Council.

    Shellharbour Airport
    Shellharbour Airport  is  a  security-controlled  licensed  aerodrome  operated  under  the Civil Aviation  Safety Authority
    (CASA).  Shellharbour Airport remains complaint with Civil Aviation Safety Authority and other regulatory requirements.

    The     Airport      Business      Park      is      nearing       completion       with      the      final      levels       and      surface      now      complete       with      the      only
    outstanding task being the installation of a permanent fence. The Airport Master Plan Expression of Interest process
    has been completed and an aviation consultant appointed to develop a new Airport Master Plan. Three workshops
    were conducted with tenants and operators to provide an opportunity for input into the plan.

    Council   submitted   a   grant   application   to   the   NSW   Government,   seeking   financial   assistance   for   the   development
    of the Airport precinct. This project aims to provide infrastructure to enable larger aircraft to utilise the airport and
    promote further growth in the employment precinct. The outcome of this grant application is expected to be known
    early in the next financial year.



62     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 63 ===
Council  endorsed  a  12-month  licence  agreement,  with  a  commercial  operator  for  the  occupation  and  operation  of
a food and beverage service. The Cove Catering  Café is  located  within the Shellharbour Airport terminal building
and has a comprehensive breakfast and lunch menu for all to enjoy.  The Cafe has  provided further activation of the
terminal facility for customers and visitors.

Wings    Over    Illawarra    was    held    with    day    one    of    the    event    being    the    first    aviation    careers    day    with    approximately
1,500 students attending from as far away as Blacktown. Exhibitors from the Australian Defence services provided
interactive virtual reality stalls and opportunities to talk about a career in the Army, Navy or Air Force. The Defence
Forces        were        joined        by        local        flying        schools        also        offering        opportunities        and        advice        on        how        to        become        a        pilot.
The weekend condinued with some fantastic displays, from the Roulettes, Paul Bennett, historic war birds and
Shellharbour ’s own HARS team, approximately 28,000 people attended the 3-day event.

Airport    was        also    utilised    by    Dog    Force  Australia    to    train    up    and    coming    bio-security    detector    dogs.    The    special
visitors     did     well     on     their     first     visit     getting     used     to     the     airport     surrounds     and     learning     to     sniff     the     luggage     on     the
conveyor belt.

Shellharbour Beachside Holiday Park
The   Holiday   Park   achieved   an   average   net   promoter   score   (NPS)   of   73   throughout   the   financial   year,   surpassing
the  NRMA  benchmark  of  60.  NPS  measures  customer  loyalty  and  satisfaction,  measuring  the  likelihood  of  visitors
recommending the park’s offerings to others. This performance is attributed to the exceptional customer service and
the variety of guest holiday activities.

The Holiday Park Plan of Management (PoM) is in its final draft stages and is expected to be completed during the
first   quarter   of   2023/24.   In   addition   to   the   PoM,   a   Heritage   Impact   Statement   and   Community   Maps   are   currently
being developed for the park. These documents will support the park’s compliance with operational regulations and
any future development initiatives to improve customer experiences.

    Public Art Strategy
    The implementation of Councils Public Art Strategy continued through the Shellharbour Mural Festival, ‘More Things
    Like This’,  bringing  together  high-profile  mural  artists  and  local,  emerging  street  artists  to  create  four  murals  in  the
    City’s graffiti hotspots during the month of May. The mural festival provided a unique mentorship opportunity for up-
    and-coming artists to build their skills and connections, helping to expand the local talent pool for future large-scale
    artworks. This project is an expansion of Shellharbour ’s Urban Art Program, which has successfully reduced graffiti,
    increased community ownership and built a stronger sense of pride in neighbourhoods across the City.

    The completed artworks include:

    •     Still Life Blues by Alex Sugar at 155 Tongarra Road, Albion Park
    •     Locking Horns by Ling at 161 Tongarra Road, Albion Park
    •     House in the Hills by Helen Proctor at 191 Princes Highway, Albion Park Rail
    •     Unceasing Day & Evening Sky by Michael Black at Hall Lane, Warilla















    Several artworks were installed across the City as part of the Art in Unexpected Places program, this included the
    installation of new murals to enhance the facades of two of our local pools. ‘Tongarra Pass’ by David Cragg at Albion
    Park pool is an amazing piece that draws its inspiration from Macquarie Pass and the flora and fauna to be found in
    the area. MAN.De’s  vibrant  mural  at  Oak  Flats  also  depicts  local  birds  and  flowers,  bringing  colour  and  life  to  the
    pool’s entrance.

    Artists were invited to submit an expression of interest to exhibit artwork in bus shelters around the City, with a total
    of eight artist being selected to exhibit their work over the coming two years. This program showcases the work of
    one artist for three months, before rotating on to another artists’ work. This is the second year of the program, which
    has received exceptionally positive feedback from the community, brightened up our public spaces and worked to
    deter graffiti.

    Local      artists      Clare      O’Toole      and      Patrick      Shirvington      had      their      artwork      displayed      in      bus      shelters.      Clare’s      still-life
    compositions take inspiration from everyday consumer products, from the pantry to the grocery store. Clare enjoys
    recreating iconic packaging, in a unique and vibrant style.  Patrick is an accomplished children’s book illustrator
    and investigates the relationship to the natural world through drawings.  Telling stories with mythical symbols, his
    artworks encourages us to look deeper into the unknown.

    A new public artwork has also been installed as part of the Warilla Town Centre streetscape upgrade on the corner
    of Beverley Avenue and Halls Lane. The work, titled ‘Seagulls’ by artist David Doyle, acts as outdoor seating and
    creates   interaction   between   people   and   the   larger-than-life   birds,   bringing   an   element   of   fun   to   public   space.  The
    artwork has aesthetic ties to the artwork ‘Who’s got the Chippies’ by the same artist, which is installed at the Warilla
    Library.

    As part of the ‘Shellharbour Rocks The Flats’ a new mural ‘The Great Sea of Wonder’ was created at Oak Flats
    Neighbourhood Centre. The mural brings together the art forms of collage and mural making through a collaboration
    by Zoe Xuji and Chloe Watfern. The artwork aims to celebrate the river systems, ocean bays and nature walks of the
    Illawarra through abstract shapes and patterns.











66     Shellharbour City Council Annual Report 2022 - 2023=== PAGE 67 ===
‘More Things Like This’
The  official  opening  of  Council's  new  bronze  whale  art  installation,  located  at  the  eastern  end  of  Reddall  Reserve,
Lake        Illawarra        took        place        during        National        Reconciliation        Week.        Burri        Burri        (meaning        whale        in        local        Dharawal
language),    is    based    on    Gang    man    gang,    a    local    Dreaming    story    that    tells    of    the    creation    of    Windang    Island    and
how the Cultural Custodians came to be in the Illawarra. It depicts a bronze sculpture of Burri Burri, the whale, and
features two large bronze coolamons, designed to be used as seats. Burri Burri was developed by Dr. Jodi Edwards,
a local Yuin Dharawal Educator and knowledge holder, Theresa Ardler, a Gweagal Dharawal Artist and Educator and
Nicole Talbott, a Dharawal Artist, in collaboration with Sculptor Julie Squires.

    Marina Services Centre
    The Marina Services Centre and the Marina Storage area were constructed and are now
    operational.  The  Marina  Services  Centre  provides  office  space  for  the  Marina  Manager

    on the Marina Services Centre ‘green roof’. The ‘green roof’ provides one of the best
    vantage points to take in views of the magnificent Shell Cove Harbour.

    The Marina Storage area sits underneath the Waterfront Tavern and provides working

    adds to the state-of-the-art maritime facilities available to Marina customers at Shell Cove
